# GAVD sequence videos

This collection contains **656 of the 666 requested sequence videos** and retains **100 of the 103 referenced YouTube sources**. A separate check confirmed that all **656 checked clips contain exactly the requested frames**, for a total of **137,232 frames**. The collection covers the GAVD labels `cerebral palsy`, `myopathic`, `normal`, `parkinsons`, and `stroke`.

**10 requested clips are still missing.** 3 YouTube sources could not be downloaded, and the available source described below is too short for its requested sequence. Every saved clip passed the frame checks. The full inventory preserves all requested sequences, including the ones that could not be produced.

A separate review flagged **4 saved clips** because their video content appears inconsistent with the annotations. Those clips should be left out of machine-learning datasets until the reference is resolved; they are listed separately from the missing videos below.

This report was generated on **November 16, 2026, at 02:22:26 UTC** from the finished preparation record and independent verification report.

The saved clips occupy **3.920 GiB (4,208,561,684 bytes)**, and the retained full source videos occupy **0.638 GiB (685,086,285 bytes)**. These figures count the media files only. They exclude CSVs, reports, temporary downloads and small files used to record processing history.

Frame totals add the length of each sequence, so a source frame can be counted more than once when requested ranges overlap. A source is counted once per YouTube ID. None of the IDs is shared between these five gait labels.

## How the requested frames were selected

Each sequence video preserves the full downloaded image and includes every frame from the first number listed in its CSV through the last number, including both ends. “Cropped” therefore means trimmed in time here; no person bounding box or other spatial crop was applied. All 666 requested CSVs were checked for consecutive positive frame numbers, consistent sequence and video identities, the correct gait label, and unique output filenames.

For example, [cljo2wwu7001a3n6ljmqm39l6.csv](../../gavd/normal/cljo2wwu7001a3n6ljmqm39l6.csv) lists frames **16 through 111** of `3KnFt8bH3tE`. Keeping both endpoints produces **111 − 16 + 1 = 96 frames**, saved as `normal/3KnFt8bH3tE-16-111.mp4`.

![The example keeps frames 16 through 111, including both ends, to produce a 96-frame clip.](../../../scripts/assets/gavd-sequence-extraction.svg)

GAVD calls the first frame of a source video frame 1, while FFmpeg counts its first frame as position 0. The preparation script adjusts for that difference once, at extraction. It also resets the clip's playback start to zero while preserving the source frame order, image size and timing. No frames are added or dropped to change the playback rate, and the output omits audio, subtitles, data streams and source chapters.

<details>
<summary>Technical extraction and video format details</summary>

The one-based inclusive CSV range `[first_frame, last_frame]` becomes the zero-based half-open FFmpeg range `[first_frame-1, last_frame)`. The filter is:

```text
trim=start_frame=(first_frame-1):end_frame=last_frame,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS
```

The expected length is `last_frame-first_frame+1`. Each clip is an MP4 encoded with `libx264`, CRF 0, preset `fast`, `yuv420p` pixels and `-fps_mode passthrough`. The source's inherited metadata and chapters are removed with `-map_metadata -1` and `-map_chapters -1`. There is exactly one video stream.

CRF 0 preserves the decoded source pixels in the chosen `yuv420p` representation. It does not restore quality already lost before download. These files can be larger than ordinary compressed clips and can require H.264 High 4:4:4 Predictive profile support even though their pixels are `yuv420p`; FFmpeg-based readers support the files, and the example was also checked with OpenCV. Exact source and output measurements remain in the JSON reports.

</details>

## Clips whose video reference needs investigation

A separate visual review looked at still images from 100 saved source videos. For each source, it used the middle frame of the earliest completed clip. For the source that was too short to produce its requested clip, it used the middle frame of the available source video instead. Four additional source frames were examined for the video identified below. This review can reveal an obvious mismatch between a video and its annotations, but a single still cannot establish correct walking motion, person identity, bounding boxes or diagnosis throughout every clip.

**4 saved clips, containing 144 frames, should be left out of model training and evaluation until their video reference is resolved.** They come from `normal/2LbERGI34lA`, where the requested ranges show a map introduction or wide street views rather than the near-full-height walking subject described by the annotations. The clips remain saved so the discrepancy can be investigated, and their CSV files have not been changed. These clips are separate from the missing clips: their requested frames were extracted correctly, but the video content appears inconsistent with the annotation reference.

## How the saved frames were checked

The independent verifier reread the CSV files rather than trusting the preparation script's frame counts. It then opened every saved source and clip from beginning to end and compared each clip frame with the corresponding frame of its source. This checks the entire requested range, including the first and last frames, and would detect a clip shifted by one frame even if its length were correct.

The verifier also checked image dimensions, frame counts, playback timestamps and the absence of extra streams. It required the clip to begin at time zero, its frame times to increase in order, and its relative timing to stay within one millisecond of the source. Verification ran from **November 16, 2026, at 02:13:39 UTC** to **November 16, 2026, at 02:17:52 UTC**. It found **0 unexpected MP4 files** in the requested folders.

The overall verification remains incomplete because some requested videos are missing. The [independent verification report](verification.json) records the result for every sequence. The four video-reference warnings described above remain separate from this frame comparison: matching the downloaded frames does not establish that the annotations describe the right video.

The local tests deliberately include a correctly trimmed clip, a clip shifted by one frame, a clip missing its last requested frame, and a clip played at the wrong speed despite having the correct image frames. They also check missing sources, gaps in CSV frame numbers, duplicate output filenames and unexpected output videos. The recorded local regression run passed 27 tests. A separate integrity check examined all 756 saved source and clip files and found 0 differences in their file fingerprints or sizes. OpenCV 5.0.0 also opened the example clip and read all 96 frames. The [separate annotation check](.review/annotation-verification.json) records the comparison with the pinned GAVD annotation snapshot.

<details>
<summary>Technical details of the frame comparison</summary>

FFmpeg produces an MD5 fingerprint for every decoded frame in the same `yuv420p` pixel format for both source and output. The ordered output fingerprints must exactly equal the source fingerprints in `[first_frame-1:last_frame]`. Full decoding must finish without an error, and there must be exactly one output video stream. These frame fingerprints serve a different purpose from the SHA256 file fingerprints below: they compare decoded images even though the source and sequence files use different encoding and container bytes.

</details>

## Image size, playback rate and annotation coordinates

The downloader selected the best available video at or below the annotation's recorded image height and with a stated rate of at most 30 frames per second. It did not resample the video to reach that rate. The CSV files do not record the frame rate of the historical video used for annotation, so the current download cannot by itself establish exact agreement with that earlier version. The verified frame ranges are exact relative to the retained source files.

For **47 saved source videos**, at least one CSV image size differs from the downloaded image size. Bounding boxes in the CSVs remain expressed in their original coordinate system. Before using them to crop or draw over a downloaded image, account for the actual image size and check whether cropping or borders have changed the framing. This preparation did not rewrite any annotation coordinates.

An additional check compared the file fingerprints and sizes of all 100 saved sources, then compared 95 of them with older records of their YouTube formats. It found 0 file fingerprint or size differences, 0 playback-rate differences greater than 0.6 frames per second, and 0 duration differences greater than the larger of two seconds or one percent. The 5 sources without an older comparison record were `cerebral palsy/WWS-iOlLsoo`, `normal/OoCDFmCm1DE`, `normal/yFBy0X0D-w8`, `parkinsons/dxRMtNtjwCc`, `parkinsons/JUMhhwFANKE`. These older records provide useful supporting evidence, but they do not prove that the videos are identical to the versions used when GAVD was annotated.

The downloaded image proportions differ from the CSV image proportions by more than two percent for `normal/2LbERGI34lA`, `stroke/CMls0LwK5OU`, `stroke/eBvzFkcvScg`. For these videos, inspect any cropping or borders before mapping annotation coordinates, because resizing alone may not align the images. The detailed comparison is stored in the manifest and in `.review/source-metadata-audit.json`.

## Folder layout and processing history

Full source videos are saved under `data/videos-gavd/<gait_pat>/`, and the trimmed sequence videos are saved under `data/videos-ms/gavd-sequences/<gait_pat>/`. Folder names preserve the CSV's label exactly, including the space in `cerebral palsy`. Grouping full sources by gait label follows the explicit request for this task, although the repository generally prefers a single shared source directory.

```text
data/videos-gavd/
├── README.md
├── manifest.json
└── <gait_pat>/<youtube-id>.<source-container>

data/videos-ms/gavd-sequences/
├── README.md
├── manifest.json
├── verification.json
└── <gait_pat>/<youtube-id>-<first-frame>-<last-frame>.mp4
```

The source folders did not exist when this task began, so all saved originals were acquired during this session. Some JSON records say `reused_canonical` because a later step reused a pilot download or a file saved earlier in the same session. That wording does not indicate an older collection. All completed sequence clips were created during this task.

Before reusing a source, the script read it completely and counted its frames. Before reusing a clip, it required matching source, annotation and output file fingerprints. Other pretrimmed datasets were not accepted as full source videos just because their filenames resembled YouTube IDs. Downloads were checked in a temporary location before being moved to their final filenames, and incomplete or invalid files were excluded from the totals. Some interrupted pilot records lack selected-format information; the measurements made directly from the saved files are the authoritative values for this collection.

Preparation ran from **November 16, 2026, at 01:59:41 UTC** to **November 16, 2026, at 02:13:38 UTC**. It used Python **3.12.12**, yt-dlp **2026.07.04** and **ffmpeg version 8.0.1 Copyright (c) 2000-2025 the FFmpeg developers**. The Node.js version was **v25.7.0**. The recovery download for `EHx08gSNfxQ` used Node.js with yt-dlp's `ejs:github` solver after a direct stream failed. No existing Python environment or dependency lockfile was changed.

Hidden `.provenance/` folders hold the small records used for safe reruns. Source folders may also contain temporary fragments under `.acquiring/` or preserved invalid files under `.invalid/`; none is counted as an available source or completed clip. The videos remain local and ignored by Git, while the repository allows selected README files and manifests to be tracked.

## Complete sequence inventory

This table includes all **666 requested sequence CSVs**. Saved filenames link to their MP4 files, while missing filenames are shown without a video link. The reference-review column identifies the four clips that should be left out of machine-learning use pending investigation; an entry without a specific warning does not establish that all of its annotations are correct.

The [preparation record](manifest.json) provides the underlying CSV path and file fingerprint, source ID and fingerprint when available, requested frame range, expected length, processing outcome, and saved output size and fingerprint. The [verification report](verification.json) gives the independent frame-check result for each sequence.

## Annotation sources and file fingerprints

The annotation inventory is documented in [data/gavd/README.md](../../gavd/README.md). Its [source manifest](../../gavd/source/manifest.json) pins commit `a87859c881603443f200bcd640663d2c3d7a8136` of the [upstream GAVD repository](https://github.com/Rahmyyy/GAVD). Only the CSV files grouped by gait label were counted. The copies in `data/gavd/source/` contain the same annotations and were excluded to avoid double counting. No annotation records or pinned source files were changed while preparing these videos.

The reports record SHA256 values, which act as file fingerprints for detecting changes. Each annotation CSV, saved source video and completed clip has a recorded fingerprint. These values support integrity checks and repeatable processing, but they do not establish that a current YouTube video matches the historical annotation reference.

| Report | SHA256 file fingerprint |
| --- | --- |
| [Preparation record](manifest.json) | `a200edc895ba68740c3334e0508d449edfe335c1b3fefba1d823e01b18ee391d` |
| [Independent verification report](verification.json) | `9cb8cea7000fa11f6c87d0f8c51d076dd6c7dc98c5ca5ac690cd6ae995db7514` |

The [source-video report](../../videos-gavd/README.md) and its [source manifest](../../videos-gavd/manifest.json) describe the retained originals. Attribution and annotation terms are preserved in the [upstream README](../../gavd/source/UPSTREAM_README.md) and [annotation license](../../gavd/source/LICENSE). Those annotation terms do not themselves establish rights to reuse each third-party YouTube video.

Gait labels remain the dataset's annotations rather than independently confirmed diagnoses. When dividing these clips into machine-learning training and evaluation sets, keep clips and windows from the same source YouTube ID together so that one original video does not appear in both sets.

## Reproducing or updating this collection

Run these commands separately from the repository root. The existing Python 3.12 environment supplies yt-dlp and loguru, while FFmpeg and FFprobe must be on `PATH`.

```bash
experiments/multiple-sclerosis/.venv/bin/python scripts/prepare_gavd_sequence_media.py --inventory-only
experiments/multiple-sclerosis/.venv/bin/python scripts/prepare_gavd_sequence_media.py --workers 3
python3 scripts/verify_gavd_sequence_media.py --jobs 3
python3 scripts/document_gavd_sequence_media.py
```

The preparation and verification commands return a nonzero exit code while requested media remain missing or invalid. Read their reports, then run the documentation command separately; that exit code should not be treated as proof that the successfully saved clips are absent. Rerunning preparation retries unavailable sources and reuses only compatible checked files. The verifier and report generator use Python's standard library.

The focused local tests need FFmpeg and FFprobe but do not access the network:

```bash
python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts -p 'test_*gavd_sequence_media.py' -v
```

To update this generated report, edit [scripts/document_gavd_sequence_media.py](../../../scripts/document_gavd_sequence_media.py) and regenerate it from finished preparation and verification reports. This keeps the written counts and inventories tied to the recorded results.
